# iGEM Toronto 2026 — Full Project Timeline

Every dated activity of the iGEM Toronto 2026 season, from the December 2025 leads
recruitment through October 17 2026. Built by merging six source sets into one
chronological record.

## How to read this file

Events are grouped by month, then by day. Every event is one bullet on one line, in this
exact shape:

```
- [Subteam] Event description. (Source: where it came from)
```

- **Subteam** is one of: `Cross-Team`, `Leadership`, `Finance`, `Dry Lab`, `Wet Lab`,
  `Hardware`, `Human Practices`, `Venture`, `Outreach`, `Web`, `Competitions`.
- **Day headings** are `### YYYY-MM-DD (Weekday)`.
- A heading reading `### YYYY-MM (month-level, exact day unknown)` holds events known only
  to the month. These sort first within their month.
- Dates are the date the thing happened, not the date it was written down.

## Subteam definitions

| Subteam | Scope |
|---|---|
| Cross-Team | All-Hands meetings and work spanning every subteam |
| Leadership | Presidents, leads, recruitment, charter, governance, policy |
| Finance | Budget, sponsorship money, grants, fees, reimbursements, audits |
| Dry Lab | PETadex enzyme mining, machine learning pipeline, DNA synthesis design |
| Wet Lab | Assay design, cloning, biosensor and selection work, lab operations |
| Hardware | The open-source bioreactor array and the filament side project |
| Human Practices | Stakeholder interviews, regulatory, ethics, sustainability reports |
| Venture | Commercial verticals, market research, sponsorship and investor work |
| Outreach | Social media, video, mascot and character art, merch, newsletter |
| Web | Competition wiki and the club website |
| Competitions | Grand Jamboree, Tech Futures Challenge, GCM, cGEM, Mini-Jamboree, UTMiC conference, pitch competitions |

## Sources

| Source tag | What it is |
|---|---|
| `Discord #channel` | Team Discord message history, Jan 1 to Oct 17 2026, 93 channels |
| `AH #N minutes DATE` | All-Hands meeting minutes, AH #1 (Jun 8) through AH #16 (Oct 5) |
| `LM #N minutes DATE` | Leads Meeting minutes, LM #8 (Apr 29) through LM #13 (Jun 1) |
| `daily note DATE` | Purav's daily research notes |
| `Knowledge/...`, `Writing/...`, `Experiments/...` | Durable project notes in the team knowledge base |
| `Google Calendar` | Calendar events, used for meeting cadence and one-off events |
| `Dec 2025 planning sheet` | The leads planning spreadsheet covering December 2025 |

## Known limits

- **December 2025 is thin.** The Discord scrape starts January 1 2026. December comes from
  the leads planning sheet, so those entries are planned deliverables rather than confirmed
  outcomes. Winter-break transition interviews with the outgoing 2025 leads and the internal
  lead interviews happened in this window but were not logged anywhere.
- **Names are real names throughout.** Every Discord handle was mapped to a person and
  cross-checked against the team roster, so no handles remain in the event text. Where
  someone goes by a name other than their first name on the roster, the preferred name is
  given in parentheses, for example `Raffaele (Amy) William Dengler`. Members who appear
  only by first name in meeting minutes are left as first names.
- **Calendar events confirm that a meeting was scheduled**, not what happened in it. They are
  included for cadence. Where minutes exist for the same date, the minutes carry the content.
- Near-duplicate entries exist where two sources recorded the same event differently. Exact
  duplicates were removed.
- Member absences, health matters, interpersonal issues and personnel assessments were
  excluded. Finances are included deliberately; the team publishes them.

## Scale

3,506 events across 11 months, December 2025 through October 2026.

| Subteam | Events |
|---|---|
| Wet Lab | 700 |
| Outreach | 560 |
| Venture | 500 |
| Dry Lab | 481 |
| Hardware | 290 |
| Human Practices | 289 |
| Web | 239 |
| Competitions | 143 |
| Finance | 109 |
| Leadership | 107 |
| Cross-Team | 88 |

## 2025-12 (December 2025)


### 2025-12 (month-level, exact day unknown)

- [Leadership] December 2025 deliverable per the leads planning sheet: internal leads recruitment for the 2026 team. (Source: Dec 2025 planning sheet)

### 2025-12-15 (Monday)

- [Leadership] Week of Dec 15: Anthony tasked with finalizing the 2026 team charter and adding it to the Discord server, finishing the welcome message, and assembling resources (including the previous year's wiki). (Source: Dec 2025 planning sheet)
- [Leadership] Week of Dec 15: Purav and Anya tasked with creating the internal team application Google Form (with Calendly link), announcing it on Discord, with a Dec 27 deadline and internal lead interviews by Jan 3-4. (Source: Dec 2025 planning sheet)
- [Leadership] Week of Dec 15: Anthony tasked with announcing on Discord that anyone interested should prepare project pitches for the second week of January. (Source: Dec 2025 planning sheet)
- [Leadership] Week of Dec 15: project submission process defined as a Google Form requiring a detailed write-up with literature review, state of the field, and what the project would look like for each subteam. (Source: Dec 2025 planning sheet)
- [Outreach] Week of Dec 15: Outreach finishing the Instagram post for external recruitment by Sunday Dec 21, with a LinkedIn post to follow. (Source: Dec 2025 planning sheet)
- [Outreach] Week of Dec 15: Tigran assigned a promo video. (Source: Dec 2025 planning sheet)

### 2025-12-22 (Monday)

- [Leadership] Week of Dec 22: external leads and general member application Google Form to be built and sent out by Christmas. (Source: Dec 2025 planning sheet)
- [Leadership] Week of Dec 22: when2meet for the first general meeting to be sent out; the iGEM 2026 Discord server set up. (Source: Dec 2025 planning sheet)

### 2025-12-27 (Saturday)

- [Leadership] Internal team application deadline (Dec 27). (Source: Dec 2025 planning sheet)

## 2026-03 (March 2026)


### 2026-03 (month-level, exact day unknown)

- [Leadership] Weekly Leads Meetings (LM #1) began in March, running through LM #13 on June 1. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/cross-team.md status (Meeting Structure))
- [Leadership] Web was split back into its own subteam in March after a year merged with Outreach. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/leadership.md)
- [Leadership] Biweekly General Meetings were retired in late March and replaced by weekly Leads Meetings. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/leadership.md)
- [Finance] The team won $2,500 seed funding from the Tech-Futures Challenge (TFC) after a pitch video, presentation and mentor meetings. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/venture.md status 2026-04-29 (LM #8))
- [Dry Lab] The March plan for the Dry Lab ML pipeline set three stages: pretrain PlasticESM on ESM2 with LoRA, mid-tune sequence to function with an active learning loop, and post-learn RL-conditioned generation of novel sequences at 100K scale. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/DryLab-ML-Architecture.md)
- [Wet Lab] Two March brainstorming sessions produced competing assay readouts: a FACS dye-quencher assay, surface-capture enrichment, an auxotrophy selection scheme and a copper biosensor; an early genetic-code-expansion idea was dropped. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/project-overview-2026-08-13.md (Wet Lab))
- [Hardware] Hardware began in March with onboarding, a team charter and the assembly of two purchased Pioreactors as reference builds, with a Wet Lab lead joining early meetings to frame user requirements. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/project-overview-2026-08-13.md (Hardware))
- [Competitions] The team won the Global Biohackathon Judges' Choice Award in March. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/leadership.md)

- [Wet Lab] Raffaele (Amy) William Dengler explained the auxotrophy selection mechanism: a strain missing a gene for an essential metabolite can only grow (on media lacking that metabolite) if it carries a plasmid restoring that gene — analogous to antibiotic selection in bacteria; yeast conventionally uses auxotrophy, bacteria conventionally uses antibiotic resistance. - [Wet Lab] Sania Ahmad asked why copper over IPTG as inducer; Raffaele (Amy) William Dengler explained IPTG (a sugar) may decompose in the 200+°C PET 3D-printing process, whereas copper is thermally stable. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-biosensor)
- [Wet Lab] Francesca Chernous found a paper showing copper can accelerate PET degradation at >350°C, but noted the printer runs cooler so may be acceptable. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-biosensor)
- [Wet Lab] Aayush Mengane (hardware liaison) confirmed PET 3D printer runs ~260°C, below the 350°C degradation-risk threshold. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-biosensor)
- [Wet Lab] Plan set: hardware subteam to test copper salts (target: anhydrous copper sulfate) alongside other test substances (coffee, uracil) in PET printing that week; concern raised that hydrated CuSO4 could release water at high temp and damage the print. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-biosensor)
- [Wet Lab] Raffaele (Amy) William Dengler proposed a bacterial alternative to true auxotrophy: tie PET degradation to antibiotic-resistance-marker expression instead of a missing nutrient (same "break PET to live" logic as the biosensor, but resistance instead of GFP readout) — a fallback in case the yeast-based plan falls through. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-selection)
- [Wet Lab] Sania Ahmad confirmed the team will likely proceed with bacteria unless a strong case for yeast emerges. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-selection)
- [Wet Lab] Raffaele (Amy) William Dengler flagged that most anticipated assay faults are in the surface-binding assay, including possible in-cell reduction of Cu(II) to Cu(I). (Source: Discord #wet-lab-selection)
- [Wet Lab] Copper/PET chemistry discussion: team agreed copper should stay as a separate solid phase (salt/powder) rather than being melted/mixed into the PET, to avoid altering PET's melt properties or oxidation state changes. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-selection)
- [Hardware] Thomas (Thomas Quigley) + two others test the filament maker on colored PLA successfully. Plan: next formulate PETG + coffee (to grow lab's pink mold), and try melting leucine into plastic without formulation. (Source: Discord #3d-filament-project)
- [Hardware] Aayush Mengane researches leucine thermal stability: leucine vaporizes ~160°C per one paper, PET melts ≥250°C, PETG ≥230°C — concern leucine vaporizes before plastic melts, making bubbles/weak filament. Thomas revises leucine vaporization to ~150-235°C and proposes uracil (mp 340°C) as alternative since lab has uracil-knockout strains. (Source: Discord #3d-filament-project)
- [Hardware] Weekly gameplan: switch PLA→PETG Monday, add coffee to PETG midweek, write up protocol, attempt uracil melt, stain plastic with 3 dyes. (Source: Discord #3d-filament-project)
- [Human Practices] Details shared for UMichigan iGEM-hosted Bioethics Symposium. (Source: Discord #human-practices-gym)
- [Outreach] The Plastic Conference tracker was created in Google Sheets. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/iGEM-president-notes.md (Critical Decisions))
- [Web] CMS discussion continues; Abdelrahman Elshrief concludes a CMS (e.g. Strapi) likely isn't worth it since it needs a separately-hosted live server (GitHub Pages can't run it); team leans toward refining the PR-based workflow instead. (Source: Discord #web-gym)
- [Web] Jane Felicia Jonatan (Jane) introduces herself to wet lab as the wiki member building their wet lab page, asks them to flag content via this channel. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-web)

- [Hardware] A bioreactor architecture meeting debated a tester vessel versus a bacteria factory, set LCC ICCG (Tm above 90C) as the North Star enzyme with a minimum Tm above 80C, and fixed OD600 and temperature as minimum sensors. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/meeting_notes/hardware-bioreactor-2026-05-28.md)
- [Hardware] A meeting prep brief set the bioreactor's two thermal phases: E. coli growth at 37C and induction at 25C, then PET degradation at 50 to 70C, requiring a secondary heater and an in-vessel temperature ramp. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/hardware-bioreactor-meeting-prep.md)
- [Hardware] The temperature-ramp killed whole-cell workflow was chosen for the iGEM track, requiring a PETase with Tm above 75C, so HOT-PETase (Tm 82.5C) or LCC ICCG (Tm ~94C) were named as first constructs over FAST-PETase (Tm 67C). (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/hardware-bioreactor-meeting-prep.md)
- [Hardware] The OD sensor was reframed as a real-time PET degradation readout, with turbidity dropping as PET particles hydrolyse, making the bioreactor the assay platform rather than only a growth vessel. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/hardware-bioreactor-meeting-prep.md)

- [Cross-Team] A team social with snacks and games was held on Friday May 29. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/cross-team.md status 2026-05-25 (LM #12))
- [Dry Lab] ESMC SAE-feature interpretability shared; suggested rerunning classifier with SAE features; ESM embeddings UMAP ("work of art") shared, embeddings on Azure; frontend viz handoff to Alexander Leonardos. (Source: Discord #dry-lab-gym)

- [Wet Lab] Raffaele (Amy) William Dengler reviewed the practice Golden Gate assemblies and gave key feedback: never choose a Type IIS enzyme whose recognition site also appears elsewhere in the vector/inserts carried into the final product (the practice vector's AmpR contained both a BsaI and a BsmBI site, ruling both out for this vector) — otherwise the assembled product gets continually re-cut and re-ligated. (Source: Discord #cloning-for-dry-lab)
- [Wet Lab] Instructed use of Benchling's legacy Golden Gate assembly wizard, not the new one, since the new one inserts junction "spacer" sequences that shift the reading frame out of frame. (Source: Discord #cloning-for-dry-lab)
- [Wet Lab] Explained the "self-excising" enzyme-site design pattern (illustrated via the iGEM standard backbone BBa_J435300, which has 2 BsaI sites that cut themselves out of the final product) versus sites that remain in the product (the tutorial backbone) and require PCR-amplifying in new, removable sites instead. (Source: Discord #cloning-for-dry-lab)
- [Wet Lab] Confirmed the dry-lab cloning subteam does physical lab work (running PCRs and Golden Gate reactions themselves), not just computational support, alongside each member's other subteam assignment. (Source: Discord #cloning-for-dry-lab)
- [Wet Lab] Jonas Lehar asked whether the team should standardize on a single restriction enzyme for all PETase library fragments to avoid ordering multiple enzymes; Raffaele (Amy) William Dengler confirmed the actual dry-lab cloning vector (distinct from the unrelated teaching tutorial) is designed around BsmBI, so dry lab's domestication code should target that one enzyme. (Source: Discord #cloning-for-dry-lab)
- [Wet Lab] Kaitlyn Chen scheduled biosensor subteam availability (When2Meet) for two meetings: a short task-delegation meeting and a longer (~1.5-2h) research-consolidation meeting ahead of Thursday; proposed optional daily work sessions. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-biosensor)
- [Wet Lab] Raffaele (Amy) William Dengler shared Bielefeld 2015 iGEM heavy-metals biosensor reference and papers on increasing promoter sensitivity via knockout of endogenous copper transport genes; deemed not worth pursuing given time/resource constraints. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-biosensor)
- [Wet Lab] Raffaele (Amy) William Dengler recommended two key references to the subteam: one on bacteriostatic vs. bactericidal antibiotics, one on sulfa-drug resistance cassettes — sulfa drugs chosen as candidate bacteriostats because they disrupt folate biosynthesis (critical for DNA synthesis/cell division, less critical for protein synthesis when amino acids are supplied). ### 2026-06-07 (Sunday)

- [Dry Lab] AWS set up with onboarding guide; launch template (4 vCPU + 1 L40 GPU), spot instances available. (Source: Discord #dry-lab-announcements)
- [Dry Lab] Metadata-extraction output: author/paper targets for activity-data mailing list shared (spreadsheet). (Source: Discord #dry-lab-gym)
- [Wet Lab] Team agreed on control design for the biosensor/selection systems: PET mesh without copper as negative control, copper in agar as a (less useful) positive control, agar without sulfa as another positive control; agreed negative/positive controls are mandatory for every run of the assay itself (acceptable to skip during cloning only). (Source: Discord #wet-lab-selection)
- [Wet Lab] Agreed to pre-validate individual novel components before combining them: test all PET/no-PET x copper/no-copper combinations, and baseline PETase comparisons (e.g., FAST-PETase vs IsPETase vs control) — applicable across all three assays; findings to be consolidated into the master doc under "Assay Design." (Source: Discord #wet-lab-selection)
- [Wet Lab] Cindy Cheng flagged that standard LB agar is thymine-rich, which counteracts trimethoprim/sulfonamide activity (exogenous thymidine bypasses the folate-pathway block); identified CLSI-recommended cation-adjusted Mueller-Hinton agar as the standard alternative medium for TMP/sulfa susceptibility testing. Raffaele (Amy) William Dengler to add to the BOM. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-selection)
- [Hardware] Aayush Mengane clarifies team uses PETG (amorphous), not PET. Rationale for amorphous: crystallinity affects degradation rate, amorphous gives cleaner comparable results (real-world PETase app would still need crystalline PET testing). Literature: phthalic acid + copper can form Cu-oBDC, sparse, likely needs experimentation. HP stakeholder meeting requested on crystallinity. (Source: Discord #3d-filament-project)

### 2026-06-16 (Tuesday)

- [Wet Lab] Cindy Cheng raised concern: switching the induction system to TPA could bias PETase selection toward enzymes good at converting MHET to TPA specifically, rather than overall PET degradation efficiency; copper avoids this bias since it depends on physical release of particles from PET degradation rather than a specific chemical step. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-biosensor)
- [Hardware] Rebecca requests a draggable timeline feature tied to Google Docs revision history — drag to a date to see requirements as they were then, changes highlighted. (Source: Discord #hardware-web)

### 2026-06-17 (Wednesday)

- [Wet Lab] Lab schedule updated for rest of week (transformations, liquid cultures, glycerol stocks, miniprep, plate pouring); lab-work questions redirected to a dedicated channel to reduce announcement-channel clutter. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-announcements)
- [Wet Lab] Purav Gupta agreed with the TPA-biasing concern and leaned toward copper for feasibility, noting copper toxicity is controllable via copper loading in the filament. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-biosensor)
- [Wet Lab] Team compared feasibility of copper vs TPA biosensor approaches: TPA biosensors exist in literature but would require a specific strain/pathway not readily available; Cindy Cheng argued copper is more feasible overall despite the extra step of embedding it in PET, since it simplifies the rest of the assay, while TPA risks more false positives from longer degradation timescales and diffusion to neighboring cells. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-biosensor)
- [Wet Lab] Raffaele (Amy) William Dengler noted the copper-sensing plasmid is already designed. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-biosensor)

- [Wet Lab] Raffaele (Amy) William Dengler asked whether a better trimethoprim resistance marker exists or whether to switch to a sulfa drug instead, and raised ongoing concern about spontaneous antibiotic resistance as a confound. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-selection)
- [Wet Lab] Sara Habibi identified dfrB10 (a small Type II DHFR, structurally/evolutionarily distinct from chromosomal DHFRs) as the strongest TMP-resistance marker candidate, better addressing the recombination concern than dfrA1, supported by literature showing dfrB10 functions as a TMP marker in plasmid constructs. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-selection)
- [Wet Lab] Cindy Cheng shared a supporting paper and confirmed via BLAST that dfrB10 (from Pseudomonas) has no significant similarity to chromosomal sequences. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-selection)
- [Human Practices] Dr. Leroy Little Bear interview notes posted. (Source: Discord #stakeholder-interview-links-and-bios)

### 2026-06-19 (Friday)

- [Wet Lab] Kaitlyn Chen presented follow-up analysis on copper toxicity and the "cheater effect" (unresolved from a prior meeting): no toxicity issues found in surveyed papers; toxicity avoidable by tuning mesh copper concentration. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-biosensor)
- [Wet Lab] Proposed switching to CopA promoter (detects cytoplasmic copper) over CusC (detects periplasmic copper) to reduce cheater effect, since CopA requires copper to cross the inner membrane; CopA also more sensitive (detects down to 0.1mM) vs CusC (operates near toxic concentrations). (Source: Discord #wet-lab-biosensor)
- [Wet Lab] Noted CusC technically detects only Cu+, not Cu2+, but prior iGEM teams have successfully used it for Cu2+ detection regardless. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-biosensor)
- [Wet Lab] Flagged false-positive risk: copper leeching out of the PET mesh could trigger CusC/CopA without actual PETase-driven degradation; proposed mitigation is embedding copper only in an inner mesh layer, leaving the outer PET layer pure. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-biosensor)
- [Wet Lab] Suggested considering an OmpA fusion to the reporter gene to boost signal. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-biosensor)
- [Wet Lab] Cheater-effect mitigation agreed: dilute cell plating density so colonies are spaced apart, preventing copper diffusion between neighboring cells (same solution used in the selection subteam). (Source: Discord #wet-lab-biosensor)
- [Human Practices] PETadex demo during meeting. (Source: Discord #human-practices-gym)
- [Human Practices] Brainstorm doc/outline started (Clara, Megan, Komal); scheduling a call to firm up outline. (Source: Discord #regulatory-framework)
- [Human Practices] Outline drafted; split into Pt1 (how companies/countries benefit — Shrena+Manasvini) and Pt2 (economic challenges + stakeholder help — Adanna+Isabella). (Source: Discord #socioeconomic-report)
- [Human Practices] Consolidated brainstorm doc created for this year's report. (Source: Discord #sustainability-report)
- [Outreach] Planning session held; reference video shared to gauge pacing for a 2-minute runtime; storyboard doc circulated. (Source: Discord #promo-video)
- [Outreach] Script direction set: cover environmental, health, and economic impact of microplastics (one stat each), move quickly into the problem; fisherman intro concept flagged as likely needing to be scrapped; bubble/water-surface transition into animation proposed. (Source: Discord #promo-video)

### 2026-07-07 (Tuesday)

- [Dry Lab] Calendar event: iGEM DL Meeting. (Source: Google Calendar)

- [Dry Lab] In-person meeting; documentation-template reminder; folding-related paper shared; metadata spreadsheet shared. (Source: Discord #dry-lab-gym)
- [Wet Lab] Raffaele (Amy) William Dengler published fragment-design rules for ordering PETase library sequences via BsmBI Golden Gate assembly: the BsmBI recognition sequence CGTCTC and its reverse complement GAGACG are forbidden anywhere inside a fragment; the 4-nucleotide overhangs at fragment ends must match within the same assembly "bucket" and differ between buckets, must not be palindromic, and internal fragments must avoid using the CGGA/GGTG overhangs reserved for the backbone-to-first/last-fragment junction (unless deliberately doing a shared-overhang-minimal design that excludes the first/last fragment). (Source: Discord #cloning-for-dry-lab)
- [Wet Lab] Clarified CGGA/GGTG sequences are fine appearing inside a fragment — the restriction only applies to using them as an end-of-fragment overhang bridging two internal fragments. (Source: Discord #cloning-for-dry-lab)
- [Wet Lab] Urgent deadline: wet lab manifesto due EOD next day; Artem (orders approver) will not approve further orders until it's complete; Surface, Selection, and Biosensor tabs flagged as most urgent. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-announcements)

- [Wet Lab] Cindy Cheng noted TMP also requires cation-adjusted Mueller-Hinton agar since it blocks the same folate pathway as sulfonamides; flagged open question of whether using PETG instead of PET would change assay outcomes. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-selection)
- [Wet Lab] Raffaele (Amy) William Dengler noted PETG is more amorphous/pre-plasticized than crystalline PET, making enzyme access easier but less representative of real-world PET. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-selection)
- [Wet Lab] Cost comparison drove a decision to keep making competent cells in-house rather than buy them (~$350 for a tube of 20 commercial cells is expensive, and the team expects to use 100+ tubes this cycle); plan set to use existing DH5a stock and BME-available chemicals (no new order needed) to test advisor Kat's competent-cell protocol that week, run overnight to reduce burden, and buy commercial cells only when urgently short. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-subgroup-leads)
- [Wet Lab] Noted a prior attempt took ~9h to reach OD 0.15 vs. the expected ~4h to OD 0.4 (likely a media effect — LB vs. SOB have different doubling times). (Source: Discord #wet-lab-subgroup-leads)
- [Wet Lab] Confirmed sodium benzoate, copper sulfate, and competent cells had already been ordered via advisor Kat, pending Medstore delivery. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-subgroup-leads)
- [Wet Lab] A class booked the BME lab, restricting the team to back benches without stations for the day. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-announcements)
- [Wet Lab] Diagnosed an earlier ambiguous transformation result (Mix and Go vs NEB competent cells): no positive control had been run per cell type, so the team planned to rerun with proper controls. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-subgroup-leads)
- [Hardware] Rebecca outlines serial↔Arduino link: serial→Arduino control of fan/stir speed, heater intensity, pump flow in real time; Arduino→serial continuous temp/OD + actuator status. - [Venture] Prof. Allen feedback (meeting set up by Hillary): deployment is challenging because wastewater is dilute with competing organics/colloids/solids that bind PET and hinder enzyme activity; main challenge is enzyme cost-effectiveness; likely requires immobilization on a support to prevent washout. (Source: Discord #venture-wwtp)
- [Venture] Allen's suggested integration points (aligned with Rasha): secondary clarifier (PET accumulates in sludge, longer residence time), bioreactor dosing, or RAS line (higher solids may aid retention) — all need experimental validation (sludge binding, hydrophobicity/retention, enzyme half-life). (Source: Discord #venture-wwtp)
- [Venture] Allen: if WWTP use-case is demonstrated, team could commercialize, secure IP, then publish, since findings would be novel. (Source: Discord #venture-wwtp)
- [Venture] Allen recommends prioritizing PET-rich industrial wastewater or landfill leachate as alternative use-cases — higher microplastic concentration, lower flow volume, more feasible/cost-effective. (Source: Discord #venture-wwtp)
- [Venture] Technical discussion: immobilization on a high-surface-area filter/interface is likely the only way to prevent enzyme washout; team concludes enzymes cannot be recirculated like self-replicating microbes (no RAS-loop reuse) — Allen's immobilization recommendation confirmed as the path forward. (Source: Discord #venture-wwtp)
- [Outreach] Shot list finalized and shared; filming locations still need confirming; plan set for Purav to announce needed participants via team announcements. (Source: Discord #promo-video)

### 2026-07-19 (Sunday)

- [Dry Lab] A PlasticESM Stage 1 audit found the 1.11M-step ESM-C 300M LoRA run (rank 32, about 14.75M trainable parameters) trained all attention layers but saved only the out_proj adapters, so the Hub adapter petadex/esmc-300m-catalytic-lora holds 1.84M of 14.75M trained parameters (12.5%) and is unvalidated. (Source: daily note 2026-07-19)
- [Dry Lab] The fused-LoRA save and load path in the petabite repo was fixed (save_fused_lora, FusedLoRASaveCallback, resume guards) with 20 new tests so a rerun keeps the QKV and FFN adapters; the architecture doc still named ESM2 650M/3B while the code trains ESM-C 300M. (Source: daily note 2026-07-19)
- [Dry Lab] The shipped Stage 1 implementation diverged from the plan: base model ESM-C 300M rather than ESM2, masked-LM domain adaptation on the unlabeled PETadex corpus rather than supervised fine-tuning, and LoRA at r=32 with hand-rolled adapters on fused QKV and FFN modules. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/DryLab-ML-Architecture.md)
- [Dry Lab] The esmc-300m-catalytic-lora run reached 1.11M steps at batch size 16 and was pushed to HuggingFace, but the checkpoint retained only 12.5% of the trained adapter parameters and remained unvalidated. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/DryLab-ML-Architecture.md)
- [Dry Lab] A fused-LoRA diagnosis established that PEFT alone under-trains ESM-C because it only adapts out_proj (12.5% of the adapter budget) and silently discards hand-rolled adapters on the fused QKV and FFN modules at checkpoint time, explaining why the published esmc-300m-catalytic-lora artifact is unvalidated. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/Fused-LoRA-on-ESM-C.md)
- [Hardware] James: no comms work this week; if going "1 master Arduino controls 8" via I2C, has a diagram (one 9V for all Arduinos + one 5V per bioreactor); storage simpler (one device vs. per-bioreactor). Rebecca proposes building a custom drag-and-drop UI instead of Serial Studio, motivated by scale-up to 96 bioreactors; notes team has Claude/Gemini access via iGEM for AI-assisted dev. Decision: add EEPROM + MicroSD reader to purchase order 3; MVP build takes precedence over power-failure storage. (Source: Discord #data-and-controls)
- [Outreach] Dry lab filming conducted at Donnelly Centre, 6th floor. (Source: Discord #promo-video)

- [Wet Lab] Decision on the shared PD (PETase-display) plasmid: rather than redesign it now that the surface-display assay is cancelled (PD's pTac promoter was originally needed for surface inducibility), the team opted to keep PD unchanged and simply add IPTG to all assay-plate media going forward. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-subgroup-leads)
- [Wet Lab] Resolved negative-control design for promoter testing: literature shows ~10% of random DNA sequences act as weak promoters in E. coli, and a further ~60% are one mutation away from becoming functional — making a naive random spacer unreliable as a non-promoter negative control. Decided to use either a short (10-20bp) adapter or a validated terminator sequence instead (inducible promoters without inducer were ruled out as still leaky); Patricia Rea to design the adapter/select an unused terminator to avoid recombination during cloning. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-subgroup-leads)
- [Wet Lab] Finalized J23102 (over J23119/J23106) as the constitutive promoter for the GFP and antibiotic-resistance positive controls, balancing a reliably strong signal against metabolic-burden concerns that apply only to PETase expression (driven by a different, pre-existing promoter in PD, not these). (Source: Discord #wet-lab-subgroup-leads)

- [Leadership] PETase is a four-time iGEM Toronto thesis: the 2019 project, the 2021 computational enzyme design project (ProteinSolver, Rosetta, active-learning Gaussian process over 25M sequences; co-presidents Joseph Bellissimo and Tianyu Lu), a 2022 P. putida directed-evolution proposal rejected as insufficiently novel, and 2026 PetaBite. (Source: daily note 2026-08-05)

### 2026-08-06 (Thursday)

- [Dry Lab] The ESMFold2 folding stack was stood up on Compute Canada (Killarney L40S) using the Biohub transformers fork, with the 31 GB HuggingFace cache relocated to /project. (Source: daily note 2026-08-06)
- [Dry Lab] A self-contained folding pipeline (fold.py with length-banded sharding and resume, a SLURM job array, and a fleet estimator) was written for the 18,172,960 PETadex 90% identity centroids, with outputs archived to s3://petadex-protein-structures/esmfold2-centroids/. (Source: daily note 2026-08-06)
- [Dry Lab] The first 100 ESMFold2 folds landed: bf16 failed on a dtype mismatch so fp32 became the default at about 25 GB on the 46 GB L40S; the centroid set has 5.0 billion residues (median 264 aa, max 59,275 aa, 91% under 512 aa). (Source: daily note 2026-08-06)
- [Dry Lab] Folding calibration set a 1280 aa length cap (keeps 99.75% of centroids) and showed ESMFold2-Fast folds 1.78x faster (36,034 vs 66,001 GPU-hours for 18.1M sequences) for 2.05 lower mean pLDDT and 0.022 lower pTM, with 3.15 TB of storage either way. (Source: daily note 2026-08-06)

### 2026-08-11 (Tuesday)

- [Dry Lab] Calendar event: iGEM DL Meeting. (Source: Google Calendar)

- [Wet Lab] Sara Habibi extended the Validation Workflow section deadline to Friday end of day, restating the required structure (validate antibiotic selection, copper induction/PCusC activation, copper release from PET substrate, PETase-dependent survival, unexpected surviving colonies with restreak/repeat/retransform/sequencing decision criteria). (Source: Discord #wet-lab-selection)
- [Wet Lab] Assigned three members to jointly develop a plate-design/optimization section based on a grid discussed in the prior general meeting: 8 copper-concentration columns x 4 rows (no TMP + biosensor construct; no TMP + TMP-resistance construct; 50% TMP + selection construct; 100% TMP + selection construct) — due Saturday EOD, aimed at identifying the PCusC activation threshold and optimizing copper induction plus antibiotic selection strength while diagnosing copper toxicity, insufficient PCusC activation, promoter leakiness, weak resistance expression, or over-strong selection. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-selection)
- [Wet Lab] Followed up on the hypothesis-tree flowchart figure, due Friday EOD, to be finalized from the existing hypothesis-tree summary. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-selection)
- [Wet Lab] Sara Habibi confirmed a document format with Aayush Mengane (hardware liaison) and shared a reference doc for copper-concentration inspiration. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-selection)
- [Wet Lab] Purav Gupta relayed a request from the Mac iGEM team for the team's spare 2025-distribution-kit GFP-dropout yeast plasmid (kit 1, L6); Patricia Rea proposed miniprepping and handing off a resuspended tube rather than giving away the physical kit, since the team doesn't need that plasmid itself but wants to keep some stock. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-subgroup-leads)
- [Wet Lab] Follow-up search for the physical 2025 distribution-kit plates was unsuccessful (not in the expected drawer); suggested checking 4°C and -20°C storage. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-subgroup-leads)
- [Wet Lab] Whole-plasmid nanopore sequencing succeeded and confirmed sul2 and msGFP2 as usable (sul1 still failed); copper test strips confirmed copper could be loaded into and released from PET mesh. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/project-overview-2026-08-13.md (Wet Lab))

### 2026-08-13 (Thursday)

- [Cross-Team] A 2026 project overview was compiled from a full crawl of the team Discord. (Source: daily note 2026-08-13)
- [Leadership] By mid-August the team operated under charter version 2.1, IP agreements, a master task tracker, and a roster of more than 55 registered members, with Co-Presidents Purav, Anthony and Anya and Prof. Artem Babaian as PI. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/leadership.md)
- [Finance] Roughly $800K in grant applications had been submitted and were pending as of mid-August. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/leadership.md)
- [Finance] About $4,000 from academic departments was earmarked for Jamboree travel, with further applications submitted to ASSU, Skule, UTERN and SIF. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/project-overview-2026-08-13.md (Venture))
- [Dry Lab] Bioinformatics work recovered full open reading frames from truncated Logan contigs and extracted catalytic domains, producing FASTA sets of about 4.7 million catalytic cores. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/project-overview-2026-08-13.md (Dry Lab))
- [Dry Lab] An embedding Atlas built from ESM2 representations was prototyped on the PETadex website as a UMAP landscape with family and CATH-domain views. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/project-overview-2026-08-13.md (Dry Lab))
- [Dry Lab] A structure pipeline was staged to scrape AlphaFoldDB models for about 1.2 million overlapping centroids, run ESMFold broadly and reserve ColabFold, AlphaFold or Boltz for higher-quality subsets. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/project-overview-2026-08-13.md (Dry Lab))
- [Dry Lab] A metadata-propagation effort used Kraken2 to relabel reads taxonomically, feeding growth temperature and pH associations; it stalled on cluster parallelization with SRA STAT scraping proposed as an alternative. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/project-overview-2026-08-13.md (Dry Lab))
- [Dry Lab] Purav fine-tuned ESM2 and later ESM-C with LoRA on catalytic ORFs and released an esmc-300m-catalytic-lora checkpoint, framed for active learning and Bayesian optimization toward a directed-evolution reward model. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/project-overview-2026-08-13.md (Dry Lab))
- [Dry Lab] Dry Lab infrastructure consolidated onto AWS after an interim on Azure, with S3 storage, a HuggingFace organization and standardized IAM roles and launch templates, following $25,000 in AWS credits and dedicated space on Donnelly's sixth floor. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/project-overview-2026-08-13.md (Dry Lab))
- [Dry Lab] The team moved quickly to adopt ESMC and ESMFold2 after their public release. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/project-overview-2026-08-13.md (Dry Lab))
- [Dry Lab] The paper timeline was stated as data frozen Aug 1, drafting to mid-August, review to end of August and a preprint targeted for Sept 1, alongside enforced notebook and documentation standards. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/project-overview-2026-08-13.md (Dry Lab))
- [Wet Lab] Calendar event: Wet Lab Team Meeting + Subteam Lead Meeting. (Source: Google Calendar)
- [Wet Lab] Two Wet Lab meetings were held. (Source: daily note 2026-08-13)
- [Wet Lab] Subgroup-leads meeting held at 5pm in room MY763, after initial confusion over room/attendance; Sara Habibi delayed after going to the wrong room. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-subgroup-leads)
- [Wet Lab] Key Wet Lab design choices were settled: trimethoprim with dfrB10 and sulfonamide with sul1/sul2 markers, Cation-Adjusted Mueller-Hinton agar, msGFP2 as reporter, copper phthalate delivery, and pJUMP-derived p15A (AS) and ColE1 (PD) backbones assembled by Golden Gate. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/project-overview-2026-08-13.md (Wet Lab))
- [Wet Lab] PETase controls including IsPETase, FAST-PETase and Logan-preprint variants were obtained from the Brown lab. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/project-overview-2026-08-13.md (Wet Lab))
- [Hardware] No integration session; component handoff (thermal interface material, vial) went to Lindsay instead of Soham. (Source: Discord #hardware-gym)
- [Hardware] Hardware settled into subsystem ownership across OD sensing, temperature and heating, peristaltic pumping, fan stirring, power management and data transmission, choosing to build its own OD sensor and selecting the Arduino Nano over the STM32. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/project-overview-2026-08-13.md (Hardware))
- [Hardware] Hardware tooling grew to a Jira task tracker, the igem-toronto/Bioreactor GitHub repository, Onshape CAD files and RRF paperwork for lab access. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/project-overview-2026-08-13.md (Hardware))
- [Hardware] By early August the integration sketch controlled temperature, fan and pump through serial commands with PID logic, with a host script receiving JSON from one and then two Arduinos; demonstrated capabilities were mixing, pumping, OD measurement and heating to 60C. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/project-overview-2026-08-13.md (Hardware))
- [Hardware] The stated design target was a sub-$100 bioreactor assembled in under 100 minutes, fully offline and plugging directly into a laptop and power bar. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/project-overview-2026-08-13.md (Hardware))
- [Hardware] The 3D-filament side project used a ProtoCycler to extrude PETG filament with masterbatch colorant, aiming to embed copper terephthalate or phthalate; leucine and uracil were abandoned because both vaporize below printing temperatures. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/project-overview-2026-08-13.md (Hardware))
- [Hardware] Open Hardware issues were faulty fan PWM control, pump stalling below a PWM threshold, untuned temperature PID reaching only 60C against an 80C goal, and unstandardized JSON output across reactors. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/project-overview-2026-08-13.md (Hardware))
- [Human Practices] Outreach email template shared (showcase PETadex + solicit feedback). (Source: Discord #human-practices-gym)
- [Human Practices] Meeting plan finalized: cross-report call with regulatory (Aug 19), wet lab (Aug 21); hardware questions compiled and sent; dry lab to be arranged. (Source: Discord #sustainability-report)
- [Human Practices] HP built a stakeholder-mapping sheet organized by region targeting China, India, Pakistan, Indonesia, Nigeria and Indigenous Canada, with a working target of about 25 stakeholders per member. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/project-overview-2026-08-13.md (Outreach and HP))
- [Human Practices] Interview feedback was consolidated into a tagged Google Sheets database, chosen over Notion so insights map back to individual subteams. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/project-overview-2026-08-13.md (Outreach and HP))
- [Human Practices] Human Practices was framed as an award bid with a portfolio of roughly fourteen initiatives assembled by early August and three formal reports in progress on sustainability, regulatory and socioeconomic dimensions. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/iGEM/project-overview-2026-08-13.md (Outreach and HP))
- [Human Practices] Key decisions included the rebrand to PetaBite, weekly Thursday evening HP meetings, moving the feedback database to Google Sheets, and declining an iGEM Madrid collaboration on cost grounds while accepting the Mini-Jamboree cohost. - [Venture] The commercial concept evolved in name from PETadex and PETai through PetaScale to PetaBite, framed as a B2B enzymatic solution for PET and microplastic degradation with recurring-revenue contracts preferred over a consumer product. ### 2026-09-09 (Wednesday)

- [Leadership] Calendar event: iGEM Toronto Mentorship Discussion. (Source: Google Calendar)
- [Dry Lab] Write-up and figure uploaded to Dry Lab drive (for poster). (Source: Discord #dry-lab-gym)
- [Wet Lab] Alexander Leonardos delivered a postprocessing script producing order-ready fragment data (CSV output + a naming scheme, e.g. "var2" flags a degenerate codon) for the PETase library cloning order, with and without primer tails pending confirmation on which to use. (Source: Discord #cloning-for-dry-lab)
- [Wet Lab] Design summarized: each fragment carries a BsmBI site + spacer + the appropriate 4nt overhang (full backbone overhang on the first/last layer, half an inner overhang otherwise). (Source: Discord #cloning-for-dry-lab)
- [Wet Lab] Alexander Leonardos raised a reading-frame-shift concern from the added 4nt overhangs, proposing the backbone supplies specific flanking bases (G before CGGA, GC after GGTG) to keep codons intact. (Source: Discord #cloning-for-dry-lab)
- [Wet Lab] Raffaele (Amy) William Dengler clarified the design is intentional: CGGA is the codon for the final glycine of the GS-linker (which fuses each library PETase to a cell-surface-anchor protein for surface display), so the reading frame is preserved by design and unaffected by the overhang. (Source: Discord #cloning-for-dry-lab)
- [Wet Lab] Patricia Rea flagged a new, more significant concern: internal fragment junctions (where the PETase sequence is split into separately ordered pieces) need careful handling — proposed either inserting an extra glycine between fragments (imperfect representation of the true library) or fixing the exact boundary bases across all fragment variants (sacrifices full codon optimization but removes the extra-bp problem). (Source: Discord #cloning-for-dry-lab)
- [Wet Lab] Alexander Leonardos clarified their algorithm only selects cut sites falling exactly on existing codon boundaries, resolving the inner-junction frameshift concern; but confirmed that fragment lengths do vary meaningfully across library variants (to accommodate indels across ancestral sequences) — Patricia Rea flagged this length variability as a real problem requiring further discussion, and the two scheduled a voice call to resolve it. (Source: Discord #cloning-for-dry-lab)
- [Hardware] Arya to write the stir-bar wiki blurb; peristaltic-pump 3D-print files sourced from Printables; CAD dimension notes (19×15 holes; ~50×40mm, possibly ×2). (Source: Discord #hardware-gym)

- [Dry Lab] Write-ups due EOD Friday (Google doc per project); PETadex paper pre-print to go online early next week; clean write-up + SVG figure panel needed to be on the pre-print. (Source: Discord #dry-lab-announcements)
- [Wet Lab] Gap identified: no one currently owns the PET-powder/hardware work; decision made to pair a hardware-team member with a wet-lab member to coordinate (powder had been ordered earlier for a dye-release/grinding experiment, direction since shifted toward a halo assay). (Source: Discord #wet-lab-gym)
- [Wet Lab] Major risk flagged by mentor: an estimated 30% (later revised to 50%) chance the copper-mesh/PETase assay is theoretically impossible, if PETase cannot release enough copper from the mesh to activate the copper-sensing promoter. Proposed a direct test - incubate copper-PET mesh in purified/lysate PETase, then pour the result onto a GFP-reporter bacterial lawn - targeted for end of week. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-gym)
- [Wet Lab] Secondary risk flagged: the ordered "PET" mesh might not actually be PET (possible seller error); plan proposed to confirm material identity via Raman or FTIR spectroscopy at a UofT core facility. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-gym)
- [Wet Lab] Experimental design limitation noted: copper mesh leaches more copper in liquid than in water, so a simple liquid-leaching comparison can't by itself prove enough copper is released to activate the promoter under real assay conditions. Team agrees a BHET assay is the fallback if the copper assay proves infeasible. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-gym)
- [Hardware] Team targets growing bacteria in the bioreactor that week; V2 "almost done"; goal 2-3 bioreactors built/running for V3. Tentative Friday-evening yeast-growth lab session, contingent on printed parts + supervision. (Source: Discord #hardware-gym)

- [Wet Lab] Mentor (Thomas Quigley) proposes an urgent copper-mesh degradation assay using externally-sourced FAST-PETase lysate (stable indefinitely at -80, ~1 week at -20, ~1 day at 4C), arguing it would validate the core assay concept and support a possible BHET-assay pivot if it fails. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-gym)
- [Wet Lab] Team (Patricia Rea) pushes the test back several days since current PD construct work takes priority; argues the proposed test alone wouldn't be fully decisive, since uncertainty remains about whether there's even enough copper in the mesh regardless of PETase activity, and a short delay won't be make-or-break. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-gym)
- [Wet Lab] Transformation and liquid-culture work proceeded in parallel on schedule (heat shock and plating completed); discussion of whether to PCR PD fragments again vs. proceed directly from GGA product. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-gym)
- [Hardware] Rebecca confirms the wiki Overview now has DBTL space, with version developments structured around the DBTL cycle. (Source: Discord #hardware-web)

(Source: Discord #wet-lab-gym)
- [Wet Lab] Comp-cell prep resumed despite limited -80 freezer space in BioZone; team runs only a starter culture, since comp cells are needed for serial transformations rather than bulk banking. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-gym)
- [Wet Lab] Transformation results: AS-only transformation grew well despite a rough transformation process, but both PD alone and PD+AS plates showed no growth despite an apparently clean transformation process. Lead concludes the PD plasmid is still non-functional, directs the team to re-incubate and recheck the next day, and requests fresh 20mL liquid cultures for AS plus a stocktake of remaining DH5a/BL21 competent cell inventory. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-gym)
- [Wet Lab] Outreach promo-video filming ran past the originally agreed shift end time; team continues informally and defers remaining lab work to the next day. (Source: Discord #wet-lab-gym)

### 2026-10-15 (Thursday)

- [Dry Lab] Dennis published two public PETadex corpora on Hugging Face: petadex-corpus-full (307,155,746 ORFs: PAZy 211, NR 4,723,128, Logan 302,432,407; 41.6 GB, 22 configs) and petadex-corpus-centroids90 (18,172,960 rows, one per 90% identity cluster). (Source: daily note 2026-10-15)
- [Dry Lab] Corpus profiling found per-sequence catalytic verification covers 6,619,107 ORFs (2.15%, roughly 5.9M complete), the corpus is 57% alpha/beta hydrolase with 22% beta-lactamase fold and 14.5% trypsin-like plus amidase, and melting point fields are null for all rows, so the "300 million PETases with full catalytic triads" claim does not hold. (Source: daily note 2026-10-15)
- [Dry Lab] At cluster level the corpus environments are predominantly environmental (soil 25.8%, marine 6.7%, freshwater 6.6%, sediment 5.4%), with wastewater or sludge dominant for 740,110 clusters. (Source: daily note 2026-10-15)
- [Dry Lab] An S3 audit found 580,146 AFDB structures (af_db_v260701, 242 GB), 1,430 PDB files, and about 1,100 test ESMFold2 folds, so prior predictions cover about 0.7% of the 18.17M centroids and the production fold starts from scratch; 132,010 centroids already have AFDB structures and 9,806 are duplicates. (Source: daily note 2026-10-15)
- [Dry Lab] Reconciling Dennis's model data plan against the release showed complete_catalytic_hit covers 88.59% of NR and PAZy ORFs but only 0.805% of Logan ORFs (6,510,200 ORFs total), with all 522 plasticome positive controls on the annotated NR side. (Source: daily note 2026-10-15)
- [Dry Lab] The PETai activity label set was profiled: 13,092 labels on 1,219 sequences from 696 references with a homology-aware 30% identity split, 4,492 labels on PET itself, pH on 53% and temperature on 30% of labels; only 401 labelled sequences are in the corpus and 21 in Logan, and the test fold holds only 7 negative labels. (Source: daily note 2026-10-15)
- [Dry Lab] Recommended scope for the October 21 wiki freeze: cut PlasticESM Stages 1 and 3, ship Stage 2 on off-the-shelf ESM-C embeddings with a re-stratified split, and present the fused-LoRA checkpoint loss as the Engineering Success learn block. (Source: daily note 2026-10-15)
- [Dry Lab] A dry lab model handoff brief was written as Purav took over model training; a LoRA rerun was recomputed at one epoch over 18.17M centroids being 5.43B tokens, about 14.5 h on an A100 or 4.6 h on an H100. (Source: daily note 2026-10-15)
- [Dry Lab] Dennis Zhu built the two canonical PETadex HuggingFace corpora: petadex-corpus-full with 307,155,746 ORFs (Logan 302.4M, NCBI nr 4.72M, PAZy 211) and petadex-corpus-centroids90 with 18,172,960 rows, one per 90% cluster. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/PETadex-Data-Inventory.md)
- [Dry Lab] Measurements on the centroid table: 18,163,154 distinct sequences, 90.4% Logan origin with median length 248 aa, 50.5% singletons, 132,010 centroids with their own AlphaFold DB structure and 1,485,792 complete catalytic hits of 1,519,979 annotated. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/PETadex-Data-Inventory.md)
- [Dry Lab] A caveat was recorded that only 2.4% of Logan centroids start with methionine and 52% run off the contig end, so N-terminal annotations are unreliable and many centroids are fragments. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/PETadex-Data-Inventory.md)
- [Dry Lab] An S3 structure audit across 21 buckets found prior predictions cover only about 0.7% of the 90pid centroids, with no production ESMFold2 fold and the ESM Atlas download never uploaded. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/PETadex-Data-Inventory.md)
- [Dry Lab] Purav took over model training and profiled the PETai label set: 98.5% of labels quantitative, PET itself the leading substrate at 4,287 labels, pH on 53% of labels and temperature on 30%, plus 902 melting-temperature labels. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/PETai-Label-Set.md)
- [Dry Lab] Five blocking problems were found in the label set: the test fold holds only 7 negatives, the in-house halo screen (holding 168 of 200 negatives) went entirely to train, provenance is inverted with test 63% LLM-scraped, 3,150 labels are discarded recoverably, and units are not yet a single regression target. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/PETai-Label-Set.md)
- [Dry Lab] An exact-MD5 join found only 401 of 1,219 labelled sequences in the published corpus, with just 21 appearing anywhere in the 302M-ORF Logan population, so the recommendation was to train on sequence alone and treat corpus columns as diagnostics. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/PETai-Label-Set.md)
- [Dry Lab] Dennis published the two PETadex HuggingFace repos: petadex-corpus-full (155 files, 41.6 GB, 22 Parquet configs) and petadex-corpus-centroids90 (3.9 GB, 57 columns), both public and ungated with license pending while PAZy, AlphaFold DB and NCBI redistribution terms are reviewed. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/PETadex-Corpus-Datasets.md)
- [Dry Lab] Protein language model embeddings, UMAP coordinates and all activity data were deliberately excluded from the published corpus, with activity labels kept in a separate unpublished PETai dataset. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/PETadex-Corpus-Datasets.md)
- [Dry Lab] HMM screen composition was measured across all 307M ORFs: 57.15% alpha/beta hydrolase, 22.22% DD-peptidase/beta-lactamase, 8.20% trypsin-like and 6.33% amidase signature, raising a specificity question about HMMs recruiting their parent superfamilies. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/PETadex-Corpus-Datasets.md)
- [Dry Lab] The plasticome layer was found to recover 425 distinct known PAZy plastic-degrading enzymes as exact sequence MD5 matches, identified as the cleanest positive control on the pipeline. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/PETadex-Corpus-Datasets.md)
- [Dry Lab] Purav took over Dry Lab model training from Dennis with a mission to produce a defensible measured model result for the wiki inside six days before the Oct 21 freeze. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/DryLab-Model-Handoff-Brief.md)
- [Dry Lab] Training cost was recomputed from FLOPs rather than step counts: one epoch over the 18.17M 90% centroids is 5.43B tokens and 6.52e18 FLOPs, about 14.5 hours on an A100 or 4.6 on an H100, so compute was ruled out as the constraint. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/DryLab-Model-Handoff-Brief.md)
- [Dry Lab] An NR versus Logan annotation skew was measured: catalytic domain annotation covers 88.59% of NR and PAZy ORFs but only 0.805% of Logan ORFs, with a higher pass rate among evaluated Logan ORFs, so the missingness is pipeline coverage and cannot be imputed as negative. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/DryLab-Model-Handoff-Brief.md)
- [Dry Lab] The melting_point and thermostability columns were found null for all 307,155,746 corpus rows. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/DryLab-Model-Handoff-Brief.md)
- [Dry Lab] Dennis's plan inventory was checked against the published release: NR-diamond raw hits, ancestral reconstructions, the embedding decoy set, ESMFold 30% centroid folds, the BacDive v2 pull and the 116-column biosample feature matrix did not ship, and InterProScan ran on 262,260 family representatives rather than corpus-wide. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/DryLab-Model-Data-Reconciliation.md)
- [Dry Lab] The PlasticESM LoRA adapter was confirmed trained on the 18.2M 90% centroids rather than all 307M ORFs, settling an earlier 18M versus 300M ambiguity, but the published checkpoint kept only 1.84M of 14.75M trained adapter parameters and recovery is closed. (Source: Knowledge/PetaScale/DryLab-Model-Data-Reconciliation.md)
- [Hardware] Claire tests whether protoboard, fan, heater, pumps physically fit the current CAD. OD calibration reverted to normalized OD (no OD600 standards on hand); exploring Claire's yeast OD curve as a substitute reference. Bioreactor run at 37°C with 20mL E. coli culture; temperature control stable to ±0.1°C (vs ±0.5°C sensor uncertainty). OD showed a spurious jump from the table being bumped; after excluding those points, an initial dip (settling) then rise (growth) — absolute calibration off but relative change promising. Follow-ups: secure loose LED connection with tape; glue + cure fan magnets before stirring (fallback to old fan); request 30mL culture; have Kat take spectrophotometer readings before/after; keep a photo/video lab notebook. (Source: Discord #hardware-gym)
- [Hardware] Rebecca asks Claire for default/dark/blank reference values as fallback if the E. coli calibration fails; requests availability 9am-12pm for text debugging while Soham runs the lab. Claire: references shouldn't be hardcoded since they determine normalized OD (nOD); open question on what to derive the reference from — goal to use the yeast OD curve to estimate E. coli OD600. (Source: Discord #od-measurements)
- [Hardware] Rebecca provides multi-angle protoboard reference photos/video for Muneer's illustration. (Source: Discord #wiki-talks)

### 2026-10-16 (Friday)

- [Dry Lab] A 2,500 aa hard cap was set on ESMFold2 folding and the long tail was moved to H100 GPUs. Of the 3,440 60% identity centroids above the L40S length ceiling (1,740 aa), the 2,386 between 1,741 and 2,500 aa were folded full-length on H100 (Fast 32/3, chunk 32, 6 shards); the 1,054 above 2,500 aa were not folded and were listed in over_cap_2500.tsv, since above that length the memory requirement needs Fold-CP and the ORFs are mostly fusions or mis-assemblies. About 4,575 of the 90% identity centroids fall under the same cap. (Source: daily note 2026-10-16)
- [Dry Lab] The 60% identity centroid ESMFold2 fold finished: 1,793,781 structures over 851.6 GPU-hours, with every input sequence having a verified structure and none failing to fold. 123,940 Full (under 150 aa) and 1,667,455 Fast (150 to 1,740 aa) finished on L40S about 14.5 hours after launch, and the 2,386 long Fast folds finished on H100 at 15:36. (Source: daily note 2026-10-16)
- [Dry Lab] Folding QC on 3,000 structures returned only false positives (stretched bonds in low-confidence loops, one ambiguity code), which were fixed in the checker. Five bad L40S nodes killed 59 shard jobs within seconds and four short-sequence shards timed out, all recovered without data loss. The 90% identity centroid run was blocked on file-quota and shard-balancing fixes. (Source: daily note 2026-10-16)
- [Dry Lab] Interactive LOBSTER wiki design element (lobster_fragment_map.html) shared for feedback. (Source: Discord #dry-lab-gym)
- [Hardware] Final assembly/testing at MSB. Wiring: LED ground to a different GND pin than labeled (already correctly wired); fan wires loosened in transit, reseated. Requested: get real OD600 (not just nOD) working; full OD-based control flagged as valuable but undeveloped; OD electrical noise needs physical + software mitigation. Fan magnet stirring confirmed working after gluing/curing. Bacteria-growth experiment run that afternoon with shift-based supervision (Purav, Kat, Thomas). UI: fixed temperature-slider bug (sliders not updating setpoints), merged OD/temp graphs + pH layout into a new "V2" file, PR opened. Final design images organized into a shared drive folder. (Source: Discord #hardware-gym)
